I am worried about how it looks, but the checking is just something I can't seem to stop. Every break, I end up in the bathroom checking my face in the mirror and fixing my hair around the spot to hide it. And then I'm constantly comparing myself to the girls who come through my line. I don't want to be doing all that, especially because sometimes I take so long in the bathroom that I'm late getting back to the register, but the urge is just really strong.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel the need to check your face in the mirror if you didn't worry about how the spot looks to others?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure. I guess I've never really thought about it like that, because... well, I do worry about how it looks to people. If I somehow didn't care what others thought, maybe I wouldn't feel the need to check it so much? But it's hard to say, since the worry is always just kind of there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the spot on your face actually looks uneven to others, or is it just that you feel like it does?\n\nPatient: I mean, I know other people say they don't see it when I point it out. But to me, it's just so obvious. When I look at photos or when I'm under those bright store lights, it's all I can see. So even if they say it's fine, I still feel like it's the very first thing customers and my managers notice when they look at me. That's actually why I came in... I just keep getting stuck on this tiny uneven spot around my nose and cheek, and I can't let it go.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel anxious or stressed if you couldn't check your face in the mirror, even if you didn't worry about how the spot looks to others?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. Like I said, I've never really been in a situation where I wasn't worried about how it looks to other people. It's hard to imagine what I'd feel if that worry wasn't there. I guess maybe I wouldn't be as stressed if I couldn't check, but... yeah, I'm just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety would decrease if the spot on your face were actually fixed or made even?\n\nPatient: I mean, I think so? If it was actually gone or completely even, I wouldn't have anything to worry about, right? So yeah, I guess my anxiety would definitely go down if it was just fixed.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel the need to check your face in the mirror if the spot were surgically corrected and you knew it looked even to others?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean, if I knew for a fact it was fixed and everyone thought it looked even... I guess I wouldn't need to check it. But honestly, I've been doing it so much lately, like on my breaks at work, that it's kind of just a habit now. So I don't know if I'd just stop completely right away. I really couldn't say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel the need to check your face in the mirror even if you were completely alone and there was no chance of anyone else seeing you?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I've never really thought about it like that. I guess if I was totally alone and knew nobody was coming over or anything... maybe I wouldn't need to check it as much? But like I said, it's just such a habit now. I might just do it without even thinking about it, even if no one was around. I'm honestly not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel the need to check your face in the mirror if you were convinced that the spot was completely even and normal-looking?\n\nPatient: I mean, if I actually believed it was completely normal... I guess I wouldn't need to look at it. But it's kind of hard to even imagine feeling that way right now. So... yeah, I don't really know for sure what I'd do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel the urge to check your face in the mirror if you were completely convinced that there was nothing wrong with your appearance?\n\nPatient: I really don't know. It's kind of the same thing... if I actually believed there was nothing wrong, I probably wouldn't feel the urge to check. But since I'm always at the register seeing people, or just catching my reflection in the store windows, it's hard to picture just turning off that urge completely. I'd like to think I wouldn't need to, but I just don't know for sure.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your need to check your face in the mirror is more about making sure you look okay, or is it something you feel compelled to do regardless of how you look?\n\nPatient: I think it's definitely about making sure I look okay, or trying to fix it so I look okay. Like, on my breaks at work, I always end up in the bathroom checking my face in the mirror. I'll spend time trying to fix my hair around it to hide the spot, or I'll just sit there comparing myself to the girls who come through my line at the register. Sometimes I get so caught up in checking and trying to fix it that I actually end up being late getting back to work. So yeah, it's really about trying to make it look better.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel the need to check your face in the mirror if you were under the impression that the spot had been completely removed?\n\nPatient: I think... if I really believed it was totally gone, I wouldn't need to check. But it's hard to say for sure, because I'm just so used to doing it at this point.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you check your face in the mirror because of how you feel about the spot, or because you feel an internal pressure to keep checking?\n\nPatient: Um, I think it's really just because of how I feel about the spot. Like, I don't feel some random pressure to just stare into a mirror for no reason. It's always about that specific area and wanting to see how bad it looks, or if I can hide it better before I go back out to the register. So, yeah, it's definitely about the spot itself, not just an urge to check just to check.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like checking your face in the mirror is something you do to relieve anxiety or distress?\n\nPatient: I guess so, yeah. Um, like I mentioned earlier about my breaks at work... when I'm in the bathroom checking the mirror and messing with my hair, it's usually because I'm feeling really stressed out. I start comparing myself to the girls coming through my line, and I just get this anxious feeling about how I look. So I go check to try and make myself feel better or relieve that stress before I have to face people again. But then, you know, I get stuck doing it and end up late getting back to the register. So... yeah, I think I definitely do it to try and calm my anxiety down."
